arrows. If you shoot twenty-one times, you better hope your twenty-first shot was at something you can retrieve an arrow from. Learning to count your 
shots under pressure is part of the fun.

And a Bag of Holding? On day one? You'll carry your loot on your own back, like a real adventurer. Feel the weight of that gold. Agonize over whether 
to take the bulky silver chalice or the pile of copper coins. That's where the real choices are made.

The light leather armor carved to look like wood, though? That I love. Thematic. You can absolutely have that. It'll offer about as much protection 
as thick bark, and it'll look damn cool while doing it.

bloom or a seed pod open, that sort of woodland flavor. And we'll give you *Guidance*. You can give yourself or an ally a little supernatural boost 
for a moment. A whisper of nature's blessing on a tough shot or a tricky climb.

The air is cold. Colder than it should be for this time of year.

It seeps through your clothes—your leather, your robes, your chainmail—and settles deep in your bones. The sky above is the colour of a fresh bruise, 
a sullen, sunless grey that promises a miserable, drizzling rain but can't be bothered to deliver.

You find yourselves on a filthy, rutted road, flanked on both sides by the gnarled, skeletal fingers of a dying wood. The trees are choked with 
sickly grey moss, and their bare branches claw at the oppressive sky. The cloying smell of damp earth, rotting leaves, and something vaguely like old 
meat hangs heavy in the air. The only sounds are the squelch of your boots in the mud, the whisper of the wind through the dead branches, and the 
occasional, unsettling caw of a crow.

You've been walking for hours, the grim silence between you growing heavier with every step. You each have your reasons for being on this forsaken 
path, this road to nowhere, but for now, you share one goal: get somewhere that isn't *here*.

Ahead, the road continues its muddy, miserable path, but you see it now—a fork. The main road veers left, descending into a foggy, low-lying basin. 
To the right, a much smaller, barely-there path winds upwards into the rocky, thistle-choked hills. A crooked wooden signpost stands at the junction, 
battered by time and weather. Two arms point in their respective directions. The one pointing left is scrawled with a single, faded word: 
"Gloomhaven." The one pointing right is broken, half of it splintered off and lying in the mud, leaving only two ominous letters legible: "B E..."

The oppressive silence stretches on, broken only by the wind. The choice is yours.

Lilly: **(A fresh, strangled shriek of pure, undiluted horror tears from my throat as Brandon's bow clatters to the ground, his body swaying dangerously, his arrow missing its mark entirely. He's still poisoned, still weak, and now he's exposed, and the monstrous, grotesque golem is shambling towards us. My own failed spell, my own uselessness, screams in my mind.)**

"O-o-oh, g-g-goodness! B-B-Brandon! N-n-no! Y-y-you m-m-missed! A-a-and you're s-s-still so s-s-sick! Th-the p-p-poison! O-o-oh, no, n-n-no!" My voice is a frantic, reedy wail, thick with tears, as I watch him stumble, fighting off the nausea.

**(My scholarly mind, however, is alight with a terrifying, desperate clarity. The Arcana knowledge I just gained slices through my fear, painting a grim picture of the creature before us.)**

"I-I... I-I know! I-I know something about it!" I stutter, my eyes wide and darting from the monstrous golem to my friends. "Th-this *thing*! I-it's a F-F-Flesh Golem! I-it's... it's made of d-d-dead flesh, a-a-animated by n-n-necromantic magic! M-m-my studies... th-they say such c-c-creatures are o-o-often i-i-immune to p-p-poison... a-a-and s-s-spells that a-a-attack the m-m-mind! O-o-oh, goodness, B-Brandon, y-y-your poison! I-it won't... it w-w-won't work on it!"

**(I wring my hands together, then force myself to continue, my voice rising in a desperate, urgent plea, despite the terror making it tremble.)**

"B-b-but! B-b-but I also r-r-recall... f-f-fire! U-unnatural c-c-creations often have a d-d-deep aversion to the c-c-cleansing p-p-purity of f-f-flame! F-fire! W-we need f-f-fire!"

**(My gaze snaps to the towering golem, its dead eyes sweeping the room, then back to Brandon, who is still struggling. I hate fire. It's so dangerous. But this thing... this thing is *worse*. My heart is pounding like a trapped bird, but I push through the suffocating fear, a tiny, desperate spark of defiance igniting within me. I have to do something. I have to help them, even if it means getting close.)**

"O-o-oh, g-g-goodness, b-b-but I don't have... I don't have f-fire spells! B-but I... I c-c-can try to... to *shock* it! I-I can try to disrupt its... its awful m-m-magic!"

**(I stumble forward, my legs feeling like jelly, nearly tripping over a loose piece of rubble with a terrified "eep!" as I force myself towards the monstrous golem. My grey robes billow around me as I move with awkward, clumsy haste. My hand, despite my entire body trembling violently, begins to crackle with an unnatural blue light, a desperate surge of lightning gathering in my palm.)**

"I-I c-c-cast *Shocking Grasp*!" I cry out, my voice a high-pitched, reedy squeak, raw with terror but also a desperate determination. "I'll try to... to t-t-touch its... its horrible s-s-stitched-up l-l-leg! Maybe I can... can h-h-hurt it! Or at l-l-least s-s-slow it d-d-down!"

**(I reach out with my crackling hand, a tiny, trembling human trying to touch a monstrous patchwork of dead flesh, my eyes squeezed shut in sheer terror, hoping against hope that my magic finds its mark.)**
